# Decision log: Modifiers, layout, input and components

Modifiers and nodes, layout and intrinsics, drawing in the tree, input, focus, semantics, animation, Material 3, navigation, platform values. Part of the [decision log](../decisions.md): the conventions and the index by number are there. The design documents describe how things work now; each row says what was decided, why, what was rejected, and links to the section with the detail.

| # | Status | Decision | Alternatives rejected | Revert cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="d18"></a>D18 | revised by [D1151](ui-and-layout.md#d1151) | `Modifier` is an inline `SmallVec` of a built-in element enum plus `Custom(Rc<dyn ModifierNodeElement>)`; elements diff in place into `ModifierNode`s; no `composed` ([06](../06-ui.md#modifiers-modifiernode-no-composed)) | all elements as trait objects (allocates per element); a fixed-size `Copy` chain with a separate handler table | medium |
| <a id="d19"></a>D19 | current | Scoped modifiers take the scope token: `Modifier.weight(row, 1.0)` ([06](../06-ui.md#modifiers-modifiernode-no-composed)) | unchecked `Modifier.weight(1.0)` with a runtime error outside `Row`/`Column` | low |
| <a id="d20"></a>D20 | current | `Box`, `Column`, `Row` are ordinary composables over a public `Layout`/`MeasurePolicy`, as in Compose ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | built-in node kinds for `Column` and `Row` | medium |
| <a id="d67"></a>D67 | current | (agent) A measure policy places its children in the call that measures them (`scope.measure`, `scope.place`, `scope.layout`), as do layout modifiers ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | Compose's placement block run after measurement (a boxed closure per measured node, or a second trait method and a stored result per node) | low |
| <a id="d68"></a>D68 | current | (agent) Draw modifier nodes have `draw` (before the content) and `draw_after` (after it, in reverse chain order): Compose's `drawContent()` split in two ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | a continuation-passing `ContentDrawScope::draw_content()` (borrows the rest of the chain and the tree inside a node's own call) | low |
| <a id="d69"></a>D69 | current | (agent) The built-in modifiers are inherent methods of `Modifier` in `compose-ui`, with their nodes; the scope tokens live there too, with hidden constructors; `compose::foundation` re-exports ([06](../06-ui.md#modifiers-modifiernode-no-composed)) | extension traits in foundation (every use site would import them), or `Custom` elements (an `Rc` per element per pass) | low |
| <a id="d71"></a>D71 | current | (agent) State read while measuring (any policy, custom layout nodes) or drawing (custom draw nodes) is observed per node by a `ReadObserver` in the runtime, as Compose's `SnapshotStateObserver`: a write measures or draws that node again at the next frame, without recomposing. Built-in nodes aren't observed (they read no state) ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | no observation (layout followed composition only; custom layouts and scrolling couldn't read state) | medium |
| <a id="d73"></a>D73 | current | (agent) `UiTree` is the applier, and owns the text measurer, focus, pointer captures and the display list ([06](../06-ui.md#nodes)) | a separate `UiApplier` wrapping the tree (one more indirection for every node update) | low |
| <a id="d74"></a>D74 | current | (agent) Application callbacks triggered by input are queued and run after dispatch, with the tree free; `on_key_event` handlers run during dispatch (their result routes the event) and can't reach the tree ([06](../06-ui.md#input-focus-and-semantics)) | running every handler during dispatch (a handler touching the host or the tree would hit a busy applier) | low |
| <a id="d75"></a>D75 | revised by [D1657](ui-and-layout.md#d1657) | (agent) One pointer change per event; a press captures its hit path; Compose's three passes; no hover enter and exit yet ([06](../06-ui.md#input-focus-and-semantics)) | multi-pointer events grouping changes (later, with touch on Android) | low |
| <a id="d76"></a>D76 | current | (agent) Focus moves through focus targets in tree order with Tab, wrapping around; enabled clickables are focus targets, clicked by Enter or Space on key up ([06](../06-ui.md#input-focus-and-semantics)) | Compose's two-dimensional focus search (later, with arrow keys) | low |
| <a id="d77"></a>D77 | current | (agent) The root measures its children with the window's constraints loosened ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | fixed constraints (every top-level node would fill the window) | low |
| <a id="d85"></a>D85 | revised by [D1271](#d1271) | (agent) `on_focus_changed(impl Fn(bool))` passes `hasFocus` (its target, or a node below, has focus), reported on attach and on change ([06](../06-ui.md#input-focus-and-semantics)) | Compose's full `FocusState` (`isFocused`, `hasFocus`, `isCaptured`) as a struct (later, if needed) | low |
| <a id="d86"></a>D86 | current | (agent) `Modifier.semantics(merge, |config| …)` runs its closure at once and keeps the configuration as a value compared by equality; the macro memoizes closures only for the callback-taking modifiers (`clickable`, `clickable_with`, `toggleable`, `on_focus_changed`, `on_key_event`) ([03](../03-composable-macro.md#lambdas-memoized-on-their-captures)) | a stored closure compared by pointer (never `same`, so receivers never skipped); memoizing every closure in a modifier chain (broke `semantics` and user methods taking closures they run at once) | low |
| <a id="d90"></a>D90 | current | (agent) A focus request on a node without a focus target focuses the first one below it, as Compose's `FocusRequester`; Material's `TextField` merges its descendants' semantics, so its tag names the field ([06](../06-ui.md#input-focus-and-semantics)) | requiring the focus target itself (a tag on a decorated field's container couldn't take text input) | low |
| <a id="d95"></a>D95 | current | (agent) A custom modifier element is an `Rc`, so a state change that rebuilds one (the checkbox's drawing) allocates; built-in elements (now including the 48 dp touch target, `minimum_touch_target`) don't, and modifiers keep six elements inline ([06](../06-ui.md#modifiers-modifiernode-no-composed)) | built-in variants for every Material element (compose-ui would grow Material's drawing) | low |
| <a id="d100"></a>D100 | revised by [D125](lazy-and-prefetch.md#d125) | (agent) `vertical_scroll`/`horizontal_scroll` keep the offset in a `ScrollState` (state, read by the scroll's layout under observation, D71): the wheel and drags (past an 8 dp slop, cancelling clicks) and `scroll_to` scroll; no flings yet, and no `LazyColumn` (every row is composed) ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | an offset in the node only | low |
| <a id="d101"></a>D101 | current | (agent) A node's `hit_clip()` clips hit testing (a `clip`, a scroll's viewport), as Compose's graphics layers ([06](../06-ui.md#input-focus-and-semantics)) | a hard-coded check for the `clip` node | low |
| <a id="d120"></a>D120 | current | (agent) Animations are tasks on the executor writing an `Animatable`'s state each frame; a new animation interrupts the running one, which returns `AnimationEndReason::Interrupted` (no exceptions); `animate_*_as_state` launches each new target's animation from a `SideEffect`, so a spring keeps its velocity ([06](../06-ui.md#animation)) | restarting a `LaunchedEffect` keyed by the target (the cancelled animation loses its velocity) | low |
| <a id="d121"></a>D121 | current | (agent) `graphics_layer`'s block takes `&mut GraphicsLayerScope` and isn't memoized; hit testing ignores the layer's transform ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | a `Callback` (it can't hold a closure over a borrowed argument); transformed hit testing (later) | low |
| <a id="d126"></a>D126 | current | (agent) `Scaffold` gives its content the bars' padding as state its measurement writes (composed again within the frame), not by subcomposition; slots are wrapped in boxes, in a fixed order ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | subcomposition (D125) | low |
| <a id="d127"></a>D127 | superseded by [D138](ui-and-layout.md#d138) | (agent) Elevation draws no shadow yet (Material 3 mostly uses tonal elevation; the FAB and elevated cards lack their shadow) | a blurred shadow op in the display list (Skia has it; Android's `Canvas` too) | low |
| <a id="d128"></a>D128 | revised by [D187](ui-and-layout.md#d187) | (agent) Material's icons are a hand-picked subset (`icons::filled::add()`, …), each parsed once per thread from its SVG path data ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | generating all of material-icons-extended (thousands of icons, compile time) | low |
| <a id="d129"></a>D129 | superseded by [D188](ui-and-layout.md#d188) | (agent) Nested scrolling is simplified: the innermost scrollable claims a drag past the slop (its Main pass) and keeps it (Initial pass); a wheel delta's unused part goes on outward. A drag's overscroll doesn't pass to the parent | Compose's full nested-scroll connection (pre- and post-scroll at each level) | low |
| <a id="d130"></a>D130 | current | (agent) Popups and dialogs are overlay nodes of the one tree and composition, positioned in the root after layout, drawn last, hit first, and semantics roots of their own ([06](../06-ui.md#popups-and-dialogs)) | separate compositions or windows per popup (child compositions sharing the parent's locals; a native window per popup on desktop) | medium |
| <a id="d131"></a>D131 | current | (agent) A node's children are drawn, and hit, in the order their parent placed them (as Compose's), sorted only when it differs from their order ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | the children's order (a scaffold's content drew over its bars) | low |
| <a id="d133"></a>D133 | current | (agent) `ImageBitmap` is premultiplied RGBA8 pixels in an `Arc<[u8]>`, decoded by the backend (`compose_skia::decode_image`); the renderer copies them into Skia once per bitmap ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | a backend-owned image handle (`ImageBitmap` stays plain data, testable headless; one copy of the pixels per live bitmap) | low |
| <a id="d135"></a>D135 | current | (agent) A non-focusable popup's outside lets presses through to what's beneath (dismissing it on the press), as Android's `FLAG_NOT_TOUCH_MODAL` popup windows; a focusable one swallows them. Dialogs dismiss on a click (release) on the scrim ([06](../06-ui.md#popups-and-dialogs)) | swallowing every outside press (the first version) | low |
| <a id="d137"></a>D137 | superseded by [D1200](ui-and-layout.md#d1200) | (agent) `MeasureScope::max_intrinsic_width(child, height)` measures the child with an unbounded width to ask its width, then the policy measures it again for real (a change inside the child then measures the parent again): an approximation of Compose's intrinsics (each layout's own intrinsic formulas) where needed (menus) ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | full intrinsic measurement on every policy and layout modifier (later, if needed) | medium |
| <a id="d138"></a>D138 | revised by [D184](ui-and-layout.md#d184), [D524](ui-and-layout.md#d524) | (agent) Shadows are a display-list op carrying the outline, elevation, light and colors, replayed with Skia's `ShadowUtils` as Compose Desktop does; the light is in the window's pixels (not moved by transforms) ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | blurring the shape in the renderer (not Compose's look); Android will replay it with `RenderNode` elevation | low |
| <a id="d139"></a>D139 | current | (agent) `place_relative` records what it placed and mirrors it after the policy returns its width (our policies place before reporting their size); layouts that compute positions from the start (lazy rows, scrolls, Material) lay out left to right and place relatively (a lazy column aligns its items in the direction and places them as they are, as Compose's), while `Row` arranges and `Box`/`Column` align with the direction and place absolutely, as Compose's ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | mirroring in every policy by hand | medium |
| <a id="d142"></a>D142 | revised by [D450](lazy-and-prefetch.md#d450), [D701](ui-and-layout.md#d701) | (agent; the grid part revised by D450) `BoxWithConstraints` first composes for a hint of its constraints (`LOCAL_CONSTRAINTS_HINT`: the window's, lazy items', grid cells'), writes the real ones as state during measurement and recomposes its content within the frame when they differ (no subcomposition, as lazy lists); `LazyVerticalGrid` is a `LazyColumn` of rows built on it ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | subcomposition in the measure pass (Compose's `SubcomposeLayout`), a larger change to the runtime | medium |
| <a id="d143"></a>D143 | current | (agent) `pointer_input` blocks are executor tasks polled synchronously as each event reaches their node (`poll_now`), giving Compose's undispatched resumption (consumption seen in the same pass); detectors defer their callbacks until the dispatch is over, so they can touch the UI ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | a callback-only gesture API (no coroutines), or resuming the block at the next `run_ready` (consumption would come too late for the other handlers) | medium |
| <a id="d146"></a>D146 | current | (agent) Animated values compare as Kotlin's boxed floats, NaN equal to NaN (`same_value` over their vectors, and their state's policy); a NaN or infinite target is reached at the next frame without animating. `Crossfade`'s stack reads its own changes in its content, so its call site isn't recomposed by them ([06](../06-ui.md#animation)) | `PartialEq` (a NaN target relaunched its animation each pass and never went idle); asking users for total-order types | low |
| <a id="d148"></a>D148 | revised by [D1268](#d1268) | (agent) `FocusRequester::request_focus` queues the request on its node's tree, which the host handles once the tree is free (after the event's callbacks, or at the next frame), as application code can't reach the tree while it's borrowed ([06](../06-ui.md#input-focus-and-semantics)) | a synchronous request (the tree is borrowed during dispatch and composition) | low |
| <a id="d150"></a>D150 | superseded by [D816](#d816) | (agent) `keyframes` specs stay `Copy` as the other specs: the keyframes go in a per-thread table, equal ones sharing an entry, and the spec names its entry; a distinct spec's entry lives for the thread ([06](../06-ui.md#animation)) | `AnimationSpec` as `Clone` holding an `Rc` (every spec use, transitions included, cloned explicitly); a fixed-capacity array of keyframes | low |
| <a id="d172"></a>D172 | current | (agent) Layout callbacks (`on_size_changed`, `on_globally_positioned`) run after the layout, outside the tree, before drawing, so state they write recomposes and lays out within the frame; `on_globally_positioned` gets a `LayoutCoordinates` snapshot, only when it changed; a graphics layer's change alone calls it too (D1187). A layout that wrote state a measurement read lays out again at once, twice at most ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | Compose's live `LayoutCoordinates` object (needs a handle to the tree outside layout); calling the callbacks after every layout; leaving measure-time writes to the next frame (a resized list's pages a frame late) | low |
| <a id="d173"></a>D173 | current | (agent) `combined_clickable` is a custom node wrapping `clickable`'s (keys, focus, hover, semantics) whose presses go through `detect_tap_gestures`' detection; its optional handlers are `Option<Callback<dyn Fn()>>`, as Rust has no default arguments. `selectable` is `semantics { selected }` before `clickable`, as Compose's ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | a timer-driven state machine inside `ClickableNode`; `impl IntoCallback` handlers with separate methods per combination | low |
| <a id="d175"></a>D175 | revised by [D210](android-drawing.md#d210), [D270](android-drawing.md#d270) | (agent) Brushes are resolved at drawing into a `Shader` kept in a table of the display list (`DisplayList::shaders`); `Paint` names it by index and stays `Copy` (its users copy it freely, Android's serializer among them). A brush paint's `color` is the gradient's average (alpha weighted), for a renderer without the shader ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | `Paint` holding an `Arc` shader (no longer `Copy`: every paint user, Material and Android's serializer, changed); a fixed-capacity inline gradient (every operation larger, an arbitrary color limit); a per-thread interning table (leaks with animated gradients) | low |
| <a id="d176"></a>D176 | revised by [D530](ui-and-layout.md#d530) | (agent) `MeasurePolicy` is implemented for measuring closures, and `measure_policy(closure)` wraps one in the `Rc<dyn MeasurePolicy>` that `Layout` takes, as a restartable composable compares its arguments. `DrawScope`'s new calls keep the scope's order (geometry, then the paint) rather than Compose's (color or brush first); `draw_oval` and `draw_circle` are whole-turn arcs; `translate` and `inset` move the scope's origin instead of recording a transform ([06](../06-ui.md#layout), [06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | a generic `Layout` (`impl IntoMeasurePolicy`: closures aren't `ComposeArg`, so it couldn't skip); Compose's parameter order for the new calls only (inconsistent within the scope) | low |
| <a id="d177"></a>D177 | current | (agent) `draw_with_cache` runs its build under a `ReadObserver` of its own (the tree's observes the node's drawing): a write to what the build read marks the cache stale and bumps a state the drawing reads, so the node draws again and builds anew; the cache is also keyed by size, density and direction ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | a new observation kind in the tree routed to the node (a capability and a hook on `ModifierNode`); building at every draw (no cache) | low |
| <a id="d180"></a>D180 | current | (agent) Material's buttons, cards and FABs take Compose's styling parameters in Compose's order (`shape`, `colors`, `elevation`, `border`, `content_padding`, `interaction_source`); colors are changed with struct update syntax (`ButtonColors { container_color, ..ButtonDefaults::button_colors() }`), so the `…Defaults` constructors take none; a default that reads another parameter (a border reading `enabled`) is an `Option` whose `None` is Compose's default, since a `#[default]` can't name another parameter ([06](../06-ui.md#material-3-components)) | `…_colors(container_color, content_color, …)` taking every color with `UNSPECIFIED` for "the default" (positional calls naming four colors to change one); a `copy` method as Compose 1.3's `ButtonColors.copy` (more code for what struct update syntax does); the border's default for enabled only (wrong when disabled) | low |
| <a id="d181"></a>D181 | current | (agent) `Surface`'s clickable and selectable overloads are `ClickableSurface(on_click, …)` and `SelectableSurface(selected, on_click, …)`, named after the modifier each adds (Rust has no overloads); the components built on them get at least the 48 dp touch target, as Compose's clickable surfaces. The toggleable overload waits for a toggleable modifier reporting to an interaction source ([06](../06-ui.md#material-3-components)) | `Surface` taking an optional `on_click` first (breaks every positional call, and can't take `selected` or `checked`); a `SurfaceClick` argument (not Compose's shape) | low |
| <a id="d182"></a>D182 | superseded by [D188](ui-and-layout.md#d188) | (agent) Nested scrolling (`NestedScrollConnection`, `Modifier.nested_scroll`) lives in material3 for now and works at the pointer's level, since foundation's scrollables don't dispatch to connections: a wheel's delta is shared exactly, a drag only estimated (the connection gets its deltas as pre-scrolls while the content scrolls by them too); a release calls the fling callbacks. To move into foundation, its scrollables dispatching `on_pre_scroll`/`on_post_scroll` to the connections around them | changing foundation's scroll and lazy lists (another agent's area at the time); intercepting drags whole in the node (the content's own drag would catch up on the next move) | low |
| <a id="d183"></a>D183 | superseded by [D1359](ui-and-layout.md#d1359) | (agent) Window insets are `PaddingValues` stand-ins (`content_window_insets`, the bars' `window_insets`), all zero by default, until the hosts report the system bars and foundation has `WindowInsets` ([06](../06-ui.md#back-saved-state-and-the-platforms-values)) | a `WindowInsets` type in material3 (would clash with foundation's); leaving the parameters out | low |
| <a id="d184"></a>D184 | revised by [D526](ui-and-layout.md#d526) | (agent) Interactive elevations snap to the state's value (Compose animates them over 90 to 150 ms) and the shadow reads the interaction source while drawing (a material3 draw node calling `DrawScope::draw_shadow`), so a hover or press draws the component again without composing it: Compose reads the animated elevation in composition, which would recompose every button on hover. The tonal elevation is the resting one, as Compose's ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | reading the state in composition (recomposes on every hover, measured in todo's recomposition test); animating with an `Animatable` per component | low |
| <a id="d185"></a>D185 | current | (agent) `ModalBottomSheet` and `SwipeToDismissBox` share an `Anchored` offset (as Compose's `AnchoredDraggableState`): anchors from the last layout, a drag clamped between them, a release settling by Compose's `computeTarget`, `confirm_value_change` able to refuse. The sheet is a focusable popup over the window, its content's scrolls dragging it through a nested-scroll connection, as Compose's; `Anchored` keys its values as `u8` (each enum's discriminant), so it's compiled once ([06](../06-ui.md#material-3-components)) | a sheet in the window's layout rather than an overlay (covered by later siblings); Compose's foundation `anchoredDraggable` (foundation lacks it) | low |
| <a id="d186"></a>D186 | revised by [D1561](#d1561) | (agent) Top app bars: one `TopAppBarLayout` measure policy for every bar, its height, the titles' alphas and the container's color read from `TopAppBarState` while measuring and drawing, so collapsing measures and draws again only; with no alignment lines, a bottom-arranged title's baseline is taken at 80 % of its height. The scroll behaviors take no arguments (state and scope remembered at the call; since D1561 `_with` and `_for` forms do, and since D1563 no scope) ([06](../06-ui.md#material-3-components)) | Compose's `TopAppBarLayout` with alignment lines (none in compose-ui); the behaviors' parameters mirrored (`state`, `canScroll`, `snapAnimationSpec`, `flingAnimationSpec`) with no default arguments to hide them | low |
| <a id="d187"></a>D187 | current | (agent) The icons are material-icons-core's 49 filled icons, in one table of labels and path data, each parsed once per thread when first asked for (D128); `NavigationRailItemColors` is `NavigationBarItemColors`, and a segmented row lays its buttons out itself without Compose's z-index ([06](../06-ui.md#material-3-components)) | a struct per item kind (duplicate code); Compose's `Row` with weights and `IntrinsicSize.Min` (the row's intrinsics are measured approximations here, D137) | low |
| <a id="d188"></a>D188 | revised by [D891](ui-and-layout.md#d891) | (agent) Nested scrolling is compose-ui's (`compose_ui::nested_scroll`, as `androidx.compose.ui.input.nestedscroll`), superseding D182: foundation's scrollables dispatch each drag, wheel and fling to the connections around them as Compose's `NestedScrollDispatcher`, finding them from the pointer's hit path (each nested-scroll node, a pointer observer, adds its connection in the initial pass), and each scrollable is itself a nested-scroll parent of those inside it, as Compose's `ScrollingLogic` ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | a parent lookup through the tree from a handler (the tree is borrowed while a node handles an event); a composition local of connections (modifiers can't provide one); keeping the pointer-level estimate (D182: a drag scrolled the content and the connection both) | medium |
| <a id="d189"></a>D189 | current | (agent) `PointerInputChange` has Compose's `previous_position` and `position_change()`: the pointer's previous event's position in the node as laid out now (from the window positions' difference), so a node that moves between events (a sheet dragged by its own `draggable`) sees the finger's move, not the finger's move less its own; `draggable` drags and tracks velocity by them ([06](../06-ui.md#layout)) | the change's position less the last one's (a dragged sheet lagged by its own move); dragging from a parent that doesn't move (not the sheet's region) | low |
| <a id="d220"></a>D220 | current | (agent) `BackHandler` registers with a host-owned `OnBackPressedDispatcher` (a composition local); back goes to the enabled handler in the most dialogs or focusable popups, then to the one registered last, as AndroidX's dispatcher. Dialogs and focusable popups take back while they show, so back never goes past them, as Compose's dialog windows; Escape that no focused node consumes goes back on every platform ([06](../06-ui.md#back-saved-state-and-the-platforms-values)) | registration order alone (a screen's handler composed after a dialog would win over it, where Compose's dialog windows get back first); keeping Escape as a key handler on the dialog (back and Escape would be two paths) | low |
| <a id="d222"></a>D222 | revised by [D1355](ui-and-layout.md#d1355) | (agent) Window insets: the host's per-kind pixel insets (`PlatformWindowInsets`, compose-ui) are a state the tree reads while measuring; foundation's `WindowInsets` is a value naming kinds and their sides (`union`, `only`), resolved where used: the paddings resolve while measuring (an insets change measures them again, nothing recomposes), `as_padding_values` in composition. No consumption (nested paddings add up), physical sides only ([06](../06-ui.md#back-saved-state-and-the-platforms-values)) | Compose's consuming paddings (a modifier local threading consumed insets down the measure pass: nodes measured alone would lose it); insets as values read in composition (every reader recomposes on keyboard changes) | low |
| <a id="d224"></a>D224 | current | (agent) Image decoding is the platform's `ImageDecoder`, one per process (`set_image_decoder`, any thread); on Android `BitmapFactory` decodes in Kotlin to a software sRGB ARGB_8888 bitmap whose premultiplied pixels Rust copies into an `ImageBitmap` (rows packed from the bitmap's stride, then the bitmap recycled), so the display list's existing image resources ship it unchanged. `remember_image_resource` is named `remember_…` because it takes a slot: `#[composable]` sees such calls in branches and groups them ([06](../06-ui.md#back-saved-state-and-the-platforms-values)) | keeping the `Bitmap` on the Kotlin side and drawing it by id (no copy back to Rust, but a second kind of image resource in serialize.rs and pixels unavailable to Rust); shipping an image codec crate | low |
| <a id="d225"></a>D225 | current | (agent) `LOCAL_CONFIGURATION` is a Rust struct of the fields apps read (screen width and height in dp, orientation, font scale, density, night mode), set by the host: Android passes its `Configuration` (`uiMode`'s night bit); desktop the window's size in dp and winit's theme. `is_system_in_dark_theme()` (foundation) reads it; examples with a `Scaffold` pick the dark color scheme from it ([06](../06-ui.md#back-saved-state-and-the-platforms-values)) | a theme local separate from the configuration; Android's whole `Configuration` mirrored | low |
| <a id="d226"></a>D226 | current | (agent) Properties and events between Kotlin and the content: `HostProps` is a map of states created on first read, of text, number or boolean values; events are a name and a text payload to one listener, posted to the main looper so it never runs inside a call into Rust. Borders follow Compose's `BorderRenderer` (new implementation, flags on): a `Brush`, the outline's bounds, Compose's width rules (strictly more than half the side fills), path differences and a clipped double stroke where Compose uses a difference clip or an offscreen clear; `BorderStroke` (now compose-ui's, re-exported by foundation) holds a `Brush` ([06](../06-ui.md#drawing)) | `Shape` as `Rc<dyn Shape>` (an allocation per shape built in composition, every chain with a shape no longer inline); a `Copy` shape with custom shapes in a registry (their lifetime unmanaged); keeping compose-rs's per-corner clamp | high |
| <a id="d1056"></a>D1056 | revised by [D1064](ui-and-layout.md#d1064), [D1184](ui-and-layout.md#d1184) | (agent, after a review; revising D1051) A clickable's `Indication::LOCAL` is read where its node is, as Compose's `useLocalIndication` (its node's `currentValueOf(LocalIndication)`): the element keeps the unresolved form (`use_local_indication`, no factory), and `emit_layout_node` sets `LOCAL_INDICATION`'s factory, read once at the layout node's place in the composition and only if a clickable (or combined clickable) uses it, on those elements before the node takes its modifier (in place; a shared combined clickable's element copied). When node-level composition locals land (MN-8), the node reads it itself and the emit-time step goes. `Modifier.indication(source, Indication::LOCAL)` still reads it where it's made (Compose's code passes `LocalIndication.current`). | <a id="d1057"></a>D1057 | revised by [D1061](ui-and-layout.md#d1061), [D1065](ui-and-layout.md#d1065) | (agent, after a review; revising D1053) Emissions are cheaper and ordered, Compose's observable behaviour kept. compose-rs's own indications (the debug indication, the state layers) listen to their source (`InteractionSource::listen`, crate-private: called back from `emit`, no task, no queue), where a collecting task cost an executor round per emission; a collector (`interactions()`) is unchanged, and `send` walks its listeners and collectors by index (no list built per emission). A node (clickable, focusable) emits through an `Emitter`: one ordered queue, its emissions sent by one task of its own (made at its first, woken for each: Compose's `coroutineScope.launch { emit(…) }` in order), and what it sends at once (`tryEmit` where it ends its interactions, and its hovers, still sent at once as D1053 has it) sends the queue first, so a release then a window exit in one frame are Release, Exit, as Compose's. Detached or reset, the node cancels the queue as Compose's node scope: what it queued isn't sent, but its fallbacks, sent at once in order, as Compose's components fall back to `tryEmit` when their scope is cancelled first: press cancellations (`invokeOnCompletion { tryEmit(cancel) }`) and focus changes (`FocusableNode.emitWithFallback`). So a press queued before the node left never reaches a collector after its cancel, and a focus's end is never dropped (the source would stay focused). compose-rs's indications track interactions by identity, as `Ongoing` (`collect_is_*_as_state`) does, so an end whose start was dropped changes nothing. The counter's click frame went from +8 % over main to about +2 % (D1060 has the later numbers) | a task per emission (D1053); hovers queued with the rest (a hover the layout finds would draw a frame late); dropping cancellations and focus changes too (a press emitted and its cancel queued would leave collectors pressed, a dropped unfocus focused); counting interactions in the indications (a dropped start then its end went to −1) | low |

| <a id="d1060"></a>D1060 | current | (agent, after a review measured the counter click +5 % over main) The built-in indications keep what's under way in fields and draw again through their host's `NodeHandle` (`invalidate_draw`, the handle given in `on_attach`), as Compose's indication nodes call `invalidateDraw`, instead of writing a snapshot state per interaction (two writes per click, half of `send`'s time; none while composing now either). A handle's request got cheaper for everyone: the tree registry and the attached-entry set hash with FNV (`compose_runtime::KeyHasher`) instead of SipHash, and the request queue keeps its capacity. `run_ready` notes the first eight tasks it polls in an array, a set past them, so a run polling a task or two (an event's emissions) allocates nothing. A node asks once until it draws (a press and its release in one batch ask once; its draw lets the next change ask); on a node nothing draws, the request leaves its group dirty until a frame places it, as any other change (D435), and wakes nothing more. A frame applies what handles asked once more after its layout's hover refresh, so a hover the layout finds draws in that frame (D1053). The read observers' keys hash with FNV too (a clickable's draw is observed), and the queue isn't taken when empty, which lost its capacity. A batched counter click measures 4.18 µs against main's 4.10 (+1.8 %, the fastest of 25 interleaved rounds each, `counter click frames, batched`, on a loaded machine). A window taking the keys, a dropdown or a plain dialog with no pane title, sends `TYPE_WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED` from its root, and the view's as one closes. The first tell with a service on from the start compares with an empty root; a service turning on later hears nothing from before it. A property changing is told at once (`SEMANTICS_AT_ONCE`), only where things are waits 100 ms, as Compose's bounds' batches. uiautomator isn't a service to send events to (`enabledServices`), as Compose's `isEnabled`. A touch from a service goes front to back through the drawing order and looks through what isn't important (`node_at`, as `hitTestSemanticsAndInteropAt`, in Rust). | <a id="d1355"></a>D1355 | current | (agent, feature window-insets; revises D222) The insets paddings consume what they pad, as Compose's: `window_insets_padding`, its shorthands, `consume_window_insets` (insets or paddings), `on_consumed_window_insets_changed`, `recalculate_window_insets` and the size modifiers are one node type, `TraversableNode` under one key (Compose's `InsetsConsumingModifierNode`): a node takes what its nearest consuming ancestor consumed, pads by the difference, and tells the nearest consuming nodes below when it changes, which measures again what pads or sizes by it. Two differences. An element's `update` has no node context in compose-rs, so what Compose does in it with the tree (telling the descendants) runs right after the composition pass (`NodeHandle::side_effect`), before the frame's layout; while unattached it only changes the value, as Compose's. A node leaving doesn't hand its descendants its own ancestor's consumption: each looks for its nearest consuming ancestor again, because compose-rs takes a removed node out of its chain during the pass and detaches it at the pass's end. With two layout nodes, one inside the other, each losing its padding in one pass, the outer's leaves first and can't reach the inner's, already out; the inner's would then hand down what the outer consumed (Compose detaches each as it applies it, the next still linked, so the walk reaches it). Within one chain the removed leave tail first and either way is right. Consumption reads no state and costs nothing where no such node is: the traversal's kinds prune every other chain; a node whose consumption didn't change doesn't walk at all (a scaffold's, which consumes nothing, doesn't visit the screen). Differences. Compose's `node` is `modifier()`: a node of compose-rs is owned by its chain (`Delegates` hold a `Box`), so an effect can't hand one node to whichever place attaches it; the place asks for a modifier, and the same effect applied at two places draws twice where Compose attaches once. `Modifier::overscroll` therefore needs no element, and `modifierUndelegatesNodeOnDetach`, `modifierDoesNotAddAlreadyAttachedNode` and `modifierIsProducingEquals` have nothing to test. `apply_to_fling` borrows the effect for its future (as `FlingBehavior`), `PerformFling` is an `Rc<dyn Fn>` since an effect may call it again. The local factory is `None` by default, where Compose's gives Android's stretch: that needs a `RenderEffect` or an `EdgeEffect` in the replayer, and comes later with StretchOverscrollIntegrationTest and the screenshots; nothing overscrolls on Android until then, as before. `vertical_scroll` without an effect reads the factory where its node is composed (D1465), and `scrollable` without one passes none, as Compose's. The lists' parameter defaults to `remember_overscroll_effect()`. Compose's `scrollableArea` isn't ported, so ScrollableAreaTest stays. In an anchored drag the velocity the effect gets back at the first or last anchor is what the fling leaves, not what it consumed: Compose's names it `consumed` and its tests expect that. | <a id="d1688"></a>D1688 | revised by [D1689](ui-and-layout.md#d1689) | (agent, `autofill-2`; revises D1680, D1684 and D1687, from the review of `autofill`) **The platform's autofill hears only of what changed, and nothing while it's off.** **Why:** D1680 walked every node in use at the end of each frame that recomposed, measured or changed semantics, comparing what autofill reads of each with the last sync's; Android installs a manager on every view (D1687), so every scroll frame, keystroke and focus move paid about 1,430 instructions per node (with a manager doing nothing: `feed_pixel` +271%, `stress_pixel` +273%, `grid_pixel` +157%, `feed_step` +107%, `list_scroll` +30%, `field_keystroke_direct` +40%, `field_focus` +31%). Compose hears of each node as it changes, comes and goes (`onSemanticsChanged`, `onPostAttach`, `onDetach`, deactivation, reuse). **Noting:** while a manager is installed and on, the tree notes a node where its semantics are forgotten (`invalidate_semantics_of`; `forget_semantics`, which attaching, detaching, deactivating and reusing run; a chain change), where it's attached (`attach_subtree`) and where it's reused with a new id (`reuse_node`, Compose's `onPostLayoutNodeReused`); a measured node (D1257 forgets its semantics) only if what it had is autofill's (a content or data type, input text, toggle state, fillable data, `OnFillData`): Compose computes nothing for a measurement, and no built-in node gains those from one (one that did would be heard of at its next invalidation). The cache check's builds (tests) compare each node's with its `apply_semantics`. **Observation:** what semantics read is observed apart (`UiTree::set_semantics_read_observer`): a write a frame makes (a lazy list's layout state, which its semantics read) invalidates them in that frame, before it draws, and asks for no other; written outside a frame, it asks for one as before. The sync computing the list's semantics made every scroll frame ask for another (`prefetch::with_autofill_a_scroll_frame_after_prefetch_asks_for_no_other_frame`); a service reading them did too. **The focus** (S1): the node losing it is resolved when `set_active_target` runs, from what the last sync knew of it (its owner's id and whether it was autofillable), as Compose's `FocusTargetNode.onDetach` clears the focus while the node is still there: a focused field or filling node removed exits. The node taking it is resolved from the tracking at the sync, its owner looked at first if noted (N1: a node that came is shown, `onPostAttach`, before it enters; a field's value changed in the frame it took the focus is told before its enter, where Compose tells the enter first). A field's "Autofill" item asks for its owner as the tracking knows it (the field runs its own code, out of its chain: its semantics can't be computed then), nothing while the platform's autofill is off. **The platform's switch:** `UiTree::set_platform_autofill_enabled`; Android's view reads `AutofillManager.isEnabled()` when it gets its host and when its window takes the focus (back from the settings), and the bridge installs the manager off until told. Off, nothing is noted nor synced, `LOCAL_AUTOFILL_MANAGER` stays the platform's and the platform still has autofill (the toolbar's item); on again, what's there is told as at install, at the next frame (the view asks for one). Compose has no switch: its listeners run and the framework drops the calls. **Semantics read between frames** (S2, on main too): a state written outside a frame (a container's handle positions, as a drag's event changes the selection) invalidated the semantics that read it at the next frame's start only; read before (a test's finder with a manual clock), a handle's `SelectionHandleInfo` was stale (the cache check's panic). `UiTree::semantics_tree` sends the snapshot's apply notifications and applies the observed writes first. (missing.md blamed a scroll; the stale read came from the drag's event.) **Nits:** the structure's children follow the nodes' children's order (Compose's `childrenInfo`), not their drawing order (N2); a value-based field's long click enters selection mode as `CoreTextField.enterSelectionMode` (`setHandleState(Selection)`): its selection handles show whatever touched it last, its cursor handle hides (N3). **Tests:** the review's 5 ignored tests pass; `fnd/autofill_tracking.rs` and hand mutants on every note and shortcut (each killed by a test); `COMPOSE_TEST_AUTOFILL=1` gives every rule a recording manager from its first frame, as on a phone (`compose_test::use_autofill` per thread; 4 tests counting semantics computations and 8 that need no manager say `false`), and the whole suite passes with it. **Cost** (retired instructions per iteration, `compare_instructions.py`; `--autofill` puts a manager on every host, `bench-autofill`): without a manager, against `origin/main` at the merge (d3a6ecb8): `feed_pixel` +0.44% (spread 3.7%), `stress_pixel` +0.34%, `grid_pixel` +0.22%, `feed_step` +0.26%, `list_scroll` +0.32%, `field_keystroke_direct` +0.45%, `field_focus` +1.09% (D1684's semantics), `field_keystroke` +5.70% (D1684's +5.4%, and the semantics tree's send of the writes at each finder's read), `first_frame_list_items` +0.29%; A text field calls it when its measurement took in its state's value (text, selection, composition), showed it anew through an output transformation reading its own state (not a password's: its semantics tell its own text, D1143; a secure field hiding its last typed character says nothing), or moved its selection there (a tap before its layout, a long press's or a handle's drag, resolved at the measurement, which their events couldn't say). A measurement changing none of those (a new width) says nothing. **A measurement tells autofill nothing** (D1688's measure hook removed): as Compose, a node changing what its semantics read as it's measured says so; what autofill hears no longer depends on whether a service kept the semantics. Noting a measured node autofill knew (the last sync's `known` and `parts`) instead cost `field_focus` +2.3% with a manager (a Material field's label animation measures its field at each frame: its semantics computed for autofill at each). Other nodes that are both layout and semantics: text's, an image's and Material's dropdown's semantics come from their updates, scrolls' and lazy layouts' read state (observed, D1688). **The autofill-only computation** (S1) observes with `observe_adding` under the node's semantics key: the full computation's reads (a scroll's position, which `apply_autofill_semantics` doesn't read) stay observed, so a write to them still says the semantics changed at once (it had replaced them: a scroll after a sync said nothing). Reads a full computation made that the node no longer makes stay observed until the next full one (a write to one forgets its semantics, needlessly). **The kept tree** (N4): `UiHost::semantics_version` moves each time a frame's `semantics_changed` is set (`mark_semantics_changed`, every site), after applying what was written since the last frame (D1688's S2); Android's `KeptSemanticsTree` builds again only when it moved since it built, or after a frame that may have changed the semantics. It had built the whole tree at every query between a change (a focus moved by an event) and the next frame, the flag staying set until then, and kept a stale tree across a write semantics read between frames (S2's drain ran only when it built). **Nits:** `compose_test::use_autofill` returns a guard that gives back what was said before when dropped (`let _autofill = use_autofill(false)`: the `before API 26` helpers left `false` for the later tests on their thread). The parts' routing keeping a part's known owner unless it came since (`Some(part) if !newly`) runs at each key; its guard is tested by a part moved to another host. `Live::note_focus`'s node noted since the last sync is tested by a node gaining `OnFillData` from a write a service's read applied, then losing the focus before the frame. A part's owner is found again only when the part is noted: a host gaining or losing `PARTS_HOST` without its parts noted keeps them (only a text field's decoration sets it, from its creation). N5: with accessibility on, a custom node's semantics are computed twice in a frame, for the sync and the service (noted only). **Tests:** the review's 3 ignored tests pass; `fnd/semantics_in_measure.rs` (13), `android/accessibility_state.rs`' kept tree built once per change; hand mutants, each killed: the field's adoption, its selection, its shown text and the password's exception, `observe_adding`, the `!newly` guard, the node noted since, the kept tree's version and the drain before it; without the field's call 9 tests fail. **Cost** (retired instructions per iteration, `compare_instructions.py` against 2053a9b7, 5 rounds): without a manager, `list_scroll` +0.08% (spread 1.3%), `feed_step` +0.07% (1.2%), `field_keystroke_direct` −0.03%, `field_focus` −0.04%, `first_frame_list_items` +0.01%; with a manager on both sides (2053a9b7 built with `bench-autofill`): `list_scroll` +0.19%, `feed_step` +0.01%, `field_keystroke_direct` +0.08%, `field_focus` −0.20%, `first_frame_list_items` −0.06%; the other spreads under 0.4%. A manager against none, now: `list_scroll` +1.73%, `feed_step` +2.07%, `field_keystroke_direct` +3.58%, `field_focus` +1.61%, `first_frame_list_items` +4.24%. | the field invalidating through its node's handle (queued: applied at the next frame, which it would ask for); comparing the field's semantics before and after each measurement (a computation per measurement); noting the measured nodes autofill knew (+2.3% on `field_focus` with a manager, accessibility still late); `observe` for the autofill-only run when an earlier full one is current (a flag per layout node, the tree's hottest struct); forgetting a node's reads once a write invalidated them (later writes would no longer say the semantics changed); bumping the version only where semantics change between frames (a site missed keeps a stale tree) | low |
